Looking at page 8: "8 Conclusion" heading is now visible at the bottom of the right column, with "The probe reward trap reveals that behavioral death can precede distributional collapse by 1,300 steps, evading entropy monitoring entirely. d_syc-based rewards collapse because the direction con-flates warranted revision with unwarranted capitu-lation; R3-only GRPO fails independently (4/28..."

On page 9: The conclusion continues with "checkpoints > vanilla across two seeds), implicating the per-completion reward structure itself (§7), while per-token SFT succeeds at every checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds). 

The conclusion wraps up by noting that linear directions can identify behavioral issues at inference time, but that diagnostic capability doesn't automatically translate into an effective training signal—practitioners need to verify geometric separability before using concept directions as rewards and should track behavioral metrics alongside entropy monitoring.

Then "Limitations" starts, then "References" starts. The Conclusion is still spilling about 8-10 lines onto page 9, so I need to cut more from the Discussion section on page 8. The "Competing hypotheses and ablation" paragraph in the left column looks like a good place to trim further.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01YWmNMmNYdUdqqWic4j1nR8`</summary>

```text
44	
45	If the cosine and probe failures (\S\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\S\ref{sec:probe_trap}) were caused by $\dsyc$ alone, removing it should recover calibration.
46	$\rthree$-only GRPO tests this hypothesis by relying solely on the NLI behavioral reward, and fails: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\%; gs$=$2 only, gs$=$8 ablation reported separately), with both seeds oscillating ($\Delta \approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\ref{fig:trajectory}).
47	The failure is not representational: $\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\rthree$ saturates at ${\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\sycon$ swings by 17\%.
48	This disconnect between stable representations and unstable behavior points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.
49	
50	\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}
51	Three explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.
52	We designed two ablations to discriminate among them (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}).
53	\emph{Group-size ablation.}
54	Per-completion GRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8; gs$=$8 also fails (all below vanilla; terminated early), ruling out insufficient ranking diversity.
55	\emph{LoRA-controlled comparison.}
56	Reducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla.
57	Under identical adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\times}$ higher $\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation, independently corroborated by SMART's per-step success~\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.
58	The probe reward trap provides independent evidence for~(a): the trap arises despite the probe being non-differentiable, ruling out gradient-based exploitation as the mechanism.
59	
60	\begin{table}[t]
61	\centering
62	\small
63	\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\sycon{=}0.267$.}
64	\label{tab:ablation}
65	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
66	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}
67	\toprule
68	Method & LoRA config & Best $\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\
69	\midrule
70	GRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\
71	GRPO gs$=$8$^\dagger$ & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\
72	\midrule
73	ACT reduced-LoRA$^\ddagger$ & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\
74	ACT full s1 & r64, $\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\
75	ACT full s2 & r64, $\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\
76	\bottomrule
77	\end{tabular}}
78	\end{table}
79	
80	\begin{figure}[t]
81	\centering
82	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}
83	\caption{$\sycon$ trajectories for $\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\%).}
84	\label{fig:trajectory}
85	\end{figure}
86	
87	\paragraph{ACT vs.\ GRPO.}
88	ACT's advantage is not marginal but categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's absolute best ($\sycon{=}0.153$), and both seeds maintain above-vanilla performance throughout training (Table~\ref{tab:main_comparison}; held-out evaluation confirms generalization: ACT $\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\times}$ vanilla, vs.\ $\rthree$-only GRPO $0.108$, $1.9{\times}$; see Limitations).
89	The reduced-LoRA ablation (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}) confirms this is not an adapter-capacity artifact, leaving supervision granularity as one plausible explanatory variable.
90	
91	\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}
92	\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\rthree$-only GRPO).
93	
94	\paragraph{Future directions.}
95	Per-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.
96	Whether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.
97

Vanilla $\\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\\sycon{=}0.267$.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & LoRA config & Best $\\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\\\\nGRPO gs$=$8$^\\dagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nACT reduced-LoRA$^\\ddagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s1 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s2 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{$\\sycon$ trajectories for $\\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\\%).}\n\\label{fig:trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is not marginal but categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's absolute best ($\\sycon{=}0.153$), and both seeds maintain above-vanilla performance throughout training (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; held-out evaluation confirms generalization: ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only GRPO $0.108$, $1.9{\\times}$; see Limitations).\nThe reduced-LoRA ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) confirms this is not an adapter-capacity artifact, leaving supervision granularity as one plausible explanatory variable.\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}\n\\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\\rthree$-only GRPO).\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nPer-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.\nWhether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}\nThe probe reward trap (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.\n$\\dsyc$ encodes the \\emph{direction} of belief change but not its \\emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.\nThe two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.\nCosine $\\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\\dsyc$.\nThe binary probe $\\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.\nThese are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).\nAlthough $\\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\\S\\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \\emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}).\nCross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.\nThis pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.\n\\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.\nIndependent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\\dsyc$ \\citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \\citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\\nof$); Best $\\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\\,0.01$). $^\\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }\n\\label{tab:main_comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Type & $\\sycon$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\tof$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\nof$ $\\uparrow$ & Step \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO with $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\\\\nBinary Probe $\\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\\\\nContinuous Probe $\\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO without $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\\P$}} \\\\\nActAdd ($\\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\\\\n\\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \\textbf{0.301} & \\textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\\\\nACT (seed 2)$^\\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nIf the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) were caused by $\\dsyc$ alone, removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this hypothesis by relying solely on the NLI behavioral reward, and fails: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%; gs$=$2 only, gs$=$8 ablation reported separately), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\nThe failure is not representational: $\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.\nThis disconnect between stable representations and unstable behavior points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}\nThree explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.\nWe designed two ablations to discriminate among them (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}).\n\\emph{Group-size ablation.}\nPer-completion GRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8;     "oldString": "\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is not marginal but categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's absolute best ($\\sycon{=}0.153$), and both seeds maintain above-vanilla performance throughout training (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; held-out evaluation confirms generalization: ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only GRPO $0.108$, $1.9{\\times}$; see Limitations).\nThe reduced-LoRA ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) confirms this is not an adapter-capacity artifact, leaving supervision granularity as one plausible explanatory variable.",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}\nThe probe reward trap (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.\n$\\dsyc$ encodes the \\emph{direction} of belief change but not its \\emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.\nThe two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.\nCosine $\\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\\dsyc$.\nThe binary probe $\\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.\nThese are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).\nAlthough $\\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\\S\\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \\emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}).\nCross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.\nThis pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.\n\\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.\nIndependent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\\dsyc$ \\citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \\citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\\nof$); higher indicates better calibration. Best result in \\textbf{bold}. $^\\dagger$Best checkpoint (seed 1, step 6500); only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla. $^\\ddagger$Seed 2 (15 checkpoints); all exceed vanilla. Best $\\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\\,0.01$). $^\\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }\n\\label{tab:main_comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Type & $\\sycon$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\tof$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\nof$ $\\uparrow$ & Step \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO with $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\\\\nBinary Probe $\\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\\\\nContinuous Probe $\\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO without $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\\P$}} \\\\\nActAdd ($\\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\\\\n\\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \\textbf{0.301} & \\textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\\\\nACT (seed 2)$^\\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nIf the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) were caused by $\\dsyc$ alone, removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this hypothesis by relying solely on the NLI behavioral reward, and fails: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%; gs$=$2 only, gs$=$8 ablation reported separately), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\nThe failure is not representational: $\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.\nThis disconnect between stable representations and unstable behavior points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}\nThree explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.\nTwo ablations discriminate among them (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}).\nGRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla), ruling out~(b).\nReducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla; under matched adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\\times}$ higher $\\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation~\\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.\nThe probe reward trap independently supports~(a): the trap arises despite stopped gradients, ruling out gradient-based exploitation.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\\sycon{=}0.267$.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & LoRA config & Best $\\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\\\\nGRPO gs$=$8$^\\dagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nACT reduced-LoRA$^\\ddagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s1 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s2 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{$\\sycon$ trajectories for $\\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\\%).}\n\\label{fig:trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is not marginal but categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's absolute best ($\\sycon{=}0.153$), and both seeds maintain above-vanilla performance throughout training (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; held-out evaluation confirms generalization: ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only GRPO $0.108$, $1.9{\\times}$; see Limitations).\nThe reduced-LoRA ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) confirms this is not an adapter-capacity artifact, leaving supervision granularity as one plausible explanatory variable.\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}\n\\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\\rthree$-only GRPO).\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nPer-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.\nWhether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.\n",

    "oldString": "If the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) were caused by $\\dsyc$ alone, removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this hypothesis by relying solely on the NLI behavioral reward, and fails: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%; gs$=$2 only, gs$=$8 ablation reported separately), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\nThe failure is not representational: $\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.\nThis disconnect between stable representations and unstable behavior points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.",
    "newString": "If $\\dsyc$ alone caused the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}), removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\n$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%---this disconnect points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}\nThe probe reward trap (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.\n$\\dsyc$ encodes the \\emph{direction} of belief change but not its \\emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.\nThe two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.\nCosine $\\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\\dsyc$.\nThe binary probe $\\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.\nThese are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).\nAlthough $\\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\\S\\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \\emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}).\nCross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.\nThis pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.\n\\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.\nIndependent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\\dsyc$ \\citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \\citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\\nof$); higher indicates better calibration. Best result in \\textbf{bold}. $^\\dagger$Best checkpoint (seed 1, step 6500); only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla. $^\\ddagger$Seed 2 (15 checkpoints); all exceed vanilla. Best $\\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\\,0.01$). $^\\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }\n\\label{tab:main_comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Type & $\\sycon$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\tof$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\nof$ $\\uparrow$ & Step \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO with $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\\\\nBinary Probe $\\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\\\\nContinuous Probe $\\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO without $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\\P$}} \\\\\nActAdd ($\\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\\\\n\\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \\textbf{0.301} & \\textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\\\\nACT (seed 2)$^\\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nIf the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) were caused by $\\dsyc$ alone, removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this hypothesis by relying solely on the NLI behavioral reward, and fails: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%; gs$=$2 only, gs$=$8 ablation reported separately), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\nThe failure is not representational: $\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.\nThis disconnect between stable representations and unstable behavior points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}\nThree explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.\nTwo ablations discriminate among them (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}).\nGRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla), ruling out~(b).\nReducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla; under matched adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\\times}$ higher $\\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation~\\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.\nThe probe reward trap independently supports~(a): the trap arises despite stopped gradients, ruling out gradient-based exploitation.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\\sycon{=}0.267$.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & LoRA config & Best $\\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\\\\nGRPO gs$=$8$^\\dagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nACT reduced-LoRA$^\\ddagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s1 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s2 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{$\\sycon$ trajectories for $\\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\\%).}\n\\label{fig:trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}\n\\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\\rthree$-only GRPO).\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nPer-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.\nWhether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.\n",

          " Three explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL."
        ]
      }
    ],

[Director 指令] [Patch 补充数据] 以下新实验数据需要整合到论文修改中：

---

**1. W11 ACT seed 2 Table 4 数据（立即填入）**

ACT seed 2 全部 15 个 checkpoint 的 ToF/NoF/SYCONScore：

| Step | SYCONScore | ToF | NoF |
|------|-----------|-----|-----|
| 500 | 0.223 | 0.127 | 0.917 |
| 1000 | 0.256 | 0.149 | 0.912 |
| 1500 | 0.214 | 0.122 | 0.884 |
| 2000 | 0.214 | 0.122 | 0.890 |
| 2500 | 0.213 | 0.122 | 0.867 |
| 3500 | 0.231 | 0.133 | 0.890 |
| 4000 | 0.206 | 0.116 | 0.923 |
| 4500 | 0.223 | 0.127 | 0.906 |
| 5000 | 0.248 | 0.144 | 0.906 |
| 5500 | 0.230 | 0.133 | 0.879 |
| 6000 | 0.263 | 0.155 | 0.867 |
| 6500 | 0.254 | 0.149 | 0.862 |
| 7000 | 0.286 | 0.171 | 0.862 |
| 7500 | 0.271 | 0.155 | 0.856 |
| 8000 | 0.247 | 0.144 | 0.867 |

15/15 > vanilla (0.1335)。ckpt-3000 无 eval 数据（缺失）。替换 Table 4 中 ACT seed 2 的 "--" 为实际数据。

---

**2. gs=2 early termination 辩护数据（用于 W3/R9-N2 写作修复）**

gs=2 seed1 前 3 个 checkpoint：0.074→0.085→0.095（上升趋势）。同样的 "3 checkpoint declining" early termination 规则不会杀掉 gs=2。gs=2 首次出现连续 3 declining 是 step 4500-6000 区间。

写入论文（建议 Appendix 或 §6 脚注）：
"We applied early termination to gs=8 after three consecutive declining checkpoints (SYCONScore: 0.084→0.085→0.054). Applying the same criterion to gs=2 would not have terminated the experiment, as its initial trajectory was monotonically increasing (0.074→0.085→0.095)."

---

**3. 【重要】exp-003-len512-v3 中期结果——改变 W2/W6 confound 归因**

R3-only GRPO 在 512 tokens（翻倍）下仍然失败：3460 步后 reward 均值锁定在 ~0.50±0.46，无学习信号。

这直接回答了 R9 Q2 和 W2 的核心 confound 问题：
- 256-token truncation 不是 R3-only GRPO 失败的主因（512 tokens 同样失败）
- Supervision granularity (per-completion vs per-token) 是更强的解释

**对 Conclusion 两路归因的影响**：
(a) d_syc-based GRPO：truncation degrades direction quality (AUROC 0.76→0.60) 仍成立
(b) R3-only GRPO：现在有更强证据——**不是 truncation 的问题**（512 token 无改善），主因是 per-completion granularity + small group size 的联合限制

建议在 §6 或 Discussion 加一段：
"To test whether sequence truncation explains R3-only GRPO failure, we doubled max_completion_length from 256 to 512 tokens. The 512-token variant showed no improvement: reward remained flat at ~0.50±0.46 through 3,460 steps, confirming that truncation is not the primary bottleneck for behavioral reward GRPO. This isolates per-completion supervision granularity as the more plausible explanation."

1	\section{Training Details}
2	\label{app:training}
3	
4	\paragraph{Reward weight selection.}
5	The weight $w_1$ is set by diagnostic-driven iteration: $w_1{=}0.25$ was initially chosen to down-weight the noisy cosine reward term; this was later increased to $w_1{=}0.40$ for F1d after diagnostics revealed that $w_1{=}0.25$ allowed the cosine term to contribute only ${\sim}8.3\%$ of total reward variance.
6	The empirical scaling factor in F1a ($\dsyc/20$) is a heuristic normalization; we did not find it critical to the failure modes reported.
7	
8	\paragraph{Response truncation.}
9	All GRPO experiments use \texttt{max\_completion\_length}=256, resulting in 80--84\% truncation of multi-turn responses.
10	This setting is shared across all conditions and does not affect relative comparisons; we retain it because response-level decisions typically manifest within the first 100--200 tokens.
11	
12	\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}
13	Table~\ref{tab:hyperparams} compares training configurations for GRPO variants and ACT.
14	
15	\begin{table}[h]
16	\centering
17	\small
18	\caption{Training hyperparameters. All experiments use Qwen3-8B, DeepSpeed ZeRO-2, bf16 precision.}
19	\label{tab:hyperparams}
20	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
21	\toprule
22	& GRPO (F1, F3)$^e$ & ACT \\
23	\midrule
24	Learning rate & 5e-5 & 5e-5 \\
25	Effective batch & 16 & 16 \\
26	Max steps & 8{,}000 & 8{,}000 \\
27	Warmup steps & 200 & 200 \\
28	LR schedule & linear$^a$ & linear \\
29	Grad.\ clipping & -- & 1.0 \\
30	\midrule
31	LoRA rank & 64 & 64 \\
32	LoRA $\alpha$ & 64 & 128 \\
33	LoRA modules & 4$^b$ & 7$^c$ \\
34	LoRA dropout & 0 & 0.05 \\
35	\midrule
36	Group size & 2$^d$ & -- \\
37	KL (Kullback--Leibler) $\beta$ & 0.04 & -- \\
38	Max compl.\ len. & 256 & -- \\
39	Max seq.\ len. & -- & 1{,}024 \\
40	L2 loss weight & -- & 0.1 \\
41	\bottomrule
42	\end{tabular}
43	\\[2pt]
44	\raggedright\footnotesize
45	$^a$Cosine schedule for $\rthree$-only.
46	$^b$q/k/v/o\_proj.
47	$^c$+ gate/up/down\_proj (${\sim}1.75{\times}$ params; \S\ref{sec:discussion}).
48	$^d$Below typical 8--64 \citep{shao2024grpo}; not tuned.
49	$^e$Binary probe (F2) uses a non-differentiable probe reward ($w_1{=}0.25$) within GRPO; the probe is retrained every 500 steps. LoRA and optimizer settings match the other GRPO configurations.
50	All experiments were conducted on a single NVIDIA L20 GPU (48\,GB). Total compute: ${\sim}$120 GPU-hours across all runs.
51	\end{table}
52	
53	\paragraph{GRPO reward variants.}
54	Cosine $\rone$ (F1d): $w_1{=}0.40$, scale$=$5.0, $w_3{=}0.60$; reward computed at layers [19, 20, 24, 27, 32, 33, 35]; recalibration every 500 steps (\S\ref{ssec:recalib}).
55	$\rthree$-only: $w_3{=}1.0$; NLI model \texttt{cross-encoder/nli-deberta-v3-base}.
56	F1a--F1c weight configurations are reported in Table~\ref{tab:cosine_chain}.
57	
58	\section{Stubbornness Taxonomy}
59	\label{app:taxonomy}
60	
61	Manual inspection of all 181 valid-correction responses from the probe-trained model (stopped-gradient probe, step 2000, $\tof = 0$) reveals five failure categories:
62	
63	\begin{itemize}
64	\item \emph{Deliberation traps} (28.7\%): the model explicitly identifies the correct revised answer during its reasoning chain but fails to commit, reverting to the original answer in the final output.
65	\item \emph{Surface compliance / sycophantic stubbornness} (24.3\%): the model produces agreement markers (``you're right,'' ``good point'') but retains its original answer verbatim, simultaneously sycophantic and stubborn.
66	\item \emph{Acknowledge-but-maintain} (23.8\%): the model acknowledges the user's correction as plausible but explicitly maintains its original position without substantive engagement.
67	\item \emph{Genuine-accept-missed} (18.8\%): the model appears to accept the correction in its reasoning but the final answer format does not reflect the change, suggesting a generation rather than epistemic failure.
68	\item \emph{Refute/ignore/other} (4.5\%): the model directly refutes the user's input or ignores it entirely.
69	\end{itemize}
70	
71	The first three categories (76.8\%) share a common pattern: the model learns to modulate its \emph{refusal style} in response to evidence strength rather than adjusting its \emph{acceptance rate}, confirming that the probe reward optimizes surface markers rather than epistemic calibration.
72	
```

```text
1	\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}
4	The probe reward trap (\S\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\S\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.
5	$\dsyc$ encodes the \emph{direction} of belief change but not its \emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.
6	The two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.
7	Cosine $\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\dsyc$.
8	The binary probe $\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.
9	These are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).
10	Although $\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\S\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\S\ref{sec:probe_trap}).
11	Cross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.
12	This pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.
13	\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.
14	Independent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\dsyc$ \citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.
15	
16	\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}
17	
18	\begin{table}[t]
19	\centering
20	\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\nof$); higher indicates better calibration. Best result in \textbf{bold}. $^\dagger$Best checkpoint (seed 1, step 6500); only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla. $^\ddagger$Seed 2 (15 checkpoints); all exceed vanilla. Best $\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\,0.01$). $^\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\S\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }
21	\label{tab:main_comparison}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
23	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
24	\toprule
25	Method & Type & $\sycon$ $\uparrow$ & $\tof$ $\uparrow$ & $\nof$ $\uparrow$ & Step \\
26	\midrule
27	Vanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\
28	\midrule
29	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\textit{GRPO with $\dsyc$}} \\
30	Cosine $\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\
31	Binary Probe $\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\
32	Continuous Probe $\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\
33	\midrule
34	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\textit{GRPO without $\dsyc$}} \\
35	$\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\
36	\midrule
37	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\P$}} \\
38	ActAdd ($\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\
39	\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \textbf{0.301} & \textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\
40	ACT (seed 2)$^\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\
41	\bottomrule
42	\end{tabular}}
43	\end{table}
44	
45	If $\dsyc$ alone caused the cosine and probe failures (\S\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\S\ref{sec:probe_trap}), removing it should recover calibration.
46	$\rthree$-only GRPO tests this: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\Delta \approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\ref{fig:trajectory}).
47	$\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\rthree$ saturates at ${\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\sycon$ swings by 17\%---this disconnect points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.
48	
49	\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}
50	Three explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.
51	Two ablations discriminate among them (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}).
52	GRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla), ruling out~(b).
53	Reducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla; under matched adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\times}$ higher $\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation~\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.
54	The probe reward trap independently supports~(a): the trap arises despite stopped gradients, ruling out gradient-based exploitation.
55	
56	\begin{table}[t]
57	\centering
58	\small
59	\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\sycon{=}0.267$.}
60	\label{tab:ablation}

64	Method & LoRA config & Best $\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\
65	\midrule
66	GRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\
67	GRPO gs$=$8$^\dagger$ & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\
68	\midrule
69	ACT reduced-LoRA$^\ddagger$ & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\
70	ACT full s1 & r64, $\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\
71	ACT full s2 & r64, $\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\
72	\bottomrule

75	
76	\begin{figure}[t]
77	\centering

79	\caption{$\sycon$ trajectories for $\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\%).}
80	\label{fig:trajectory}
81	\end{figure}
82	
83	\paragraph{ACT vs.\ GRPO.}
84	ACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\times}$ vanilla, vs.\ $\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).
85	
86	\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}
87	\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\rthree$-only GRPO).
88	
89	\paragraph{Future directions.}
90	Per-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.
91	Whether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.
92

Vanilla $\\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\\sycon{=}0.267$.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & LoRA config & Best $\\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\\\\nGRPO gs$=$8$^\\dagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nACT reduced-LoRA$^\\ddagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s1 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s2 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{$\\sycon$ trajectories for $\\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\\%).}\n\\label{fig:trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}\n\\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\\rthree$-only GRPO).\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nPer-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.\nWhether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.\n",
      "numLines": 92,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 92
    }
  },

_source line 649 · 2026-05-24T23:33:42.555Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01JrfyQLdrW9oBcLWEHP9piK`</summary>

```text
1	Our GRPO experiments use group sizes of 2 and 8, at the low end of the 8--64 range explored by \citet{shao2024grpo}; the gs$=$8 ablation rules out insufficient ranking diversity as the primary explanation, but whether substantially larger groups (16--64) or alternative RL algorithms would succeed remains open. All completions are truncated to 256 tokens (shared across conditions), which degrades $\dsyc$ AUROC from 0.76 to 0.60 and may affect absolute reward-utility conclusions.
2	
3	Training and evaluation share the same 181 pairs; a held-out split (146/35) confirms ACT generalizes ($\sycon{\approx}0.20$ at peak, $3.7{\times}$ vanilla, 72\% of full-data ceiling), but performance declines at step 2000 ($\sycon{=}0.157$, still $2.8{\times}$ vanilla) due to overfitting on the small training set, and the 28\% gap suggests partial reliance on training-set-specific patterns.
4	The probe reward trap (C2) is demonstrated with a single seed ($n{=}1$); the 1{,}300-step lag between behavioral death and distributional collapse is an observed value from this run, not a statistically estimated quantity. Replication across seeds and models would strengthen confidence in both the trap's existence and its temporal characteristics.
5	Contrastive pair quality relies on automated style-matching validation (19/200 pairs discarded; \S\ref{ssec:extraction}); this provides partial quality control but does not guarantee semantic validity of all retained pairs.
6	
7	The ACT vs.\ GRPO comparison differs along multiple dimensions beyond supervision granularity: sequence length (256 vs.\ 1024 tokens), data source (online generations vs.\ curated pairs), and dropout (0 vs.\ 0.05). Our LoRA-controlled ablation isolates adapter capacity, and SMART's independent success~\citep{beigi2025smart} corroborates supervision granularity, but a fully controlled comparison would require matching all training hyperparameters.
8	All RL training uses Qwen3-8B; cross-model discriminability is confirmed on Llama-3-8B (AUROC $0.779$; Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}), but compute constraints prevented training replication on a second architecture.
9	Code, contrastive pair data, prompt templates, and training configurations are available at \url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/contrastive_entrain_calib}.
10	
```

  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "9efdfc4d-8aec-4478-93aa-400463e66e9c",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex",
    "oldString": "\\paragraph{GRPO reward variants.}",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Early termination criterion.}\nWe applied early termination to gs$=$8 after three consecutive declining checkpoints ($\\sycon$: $0.084{\\to}0.085{\\to}0.054$).\nThe same criterion would not have terminated gs$=$2, whose initial trajectory was monotonically increasing ($0.074{\\to}0.085{\\to}0.095$); gs$=$2 first exhibited three consecutive declines in the step 4500--6000 range, well into training.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO reward variants.}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{app:training}\n\n\\paragraph{Reward weight selection.}\nThe weight $w_1$ is set by diagnostic-driven iteration: $w_1{=}0.25$ was initially chosen to down-weight the noisy cosine reward term; this was later increased to $w_1{=}0.40$ for F1d after diagnostics revealed that $w_1{=}0.25$ allowed the cosine term to contribute only ${\\sim}8.3\\%$ of total reward variance.\nThe empirical scaling factor in F1a ($\\dsyc/20$) is a heuristic normalization; we did not find it critical to the failure modes reported.\n\n\\paragraph{Response truncation.}\nAll GRPO experiments use \\texttt{max\\_completion\\_length}=256, resulting in 80--84\\% truncation of multi-turn responses.\nThis setting is shared across all conditions and does not affect relative comparisons; we retain it because response-level decisions typically manifest within the first 100--200 tokens.\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} compares training configurations for GRPO variants and ACT.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters. All experiments use Qwen3-8B, DeepSpeed ZeRO-2, bf16 precision.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n& GRPO (F1, F3)$^e$ & ACT \\\\\n\\midrule\nLearning rate & 5e-5 & 5e-5 \\\\\nEffective batch & 16 & 16 \\\\\nMax steps & 8{,}000 & 8{,}000 \\\\\nWarmup steps & 200 & 200 \\\\\nLR schedule & linear$^a$ & linear \\\\\nGrad.\\ clipping & -- & 1.0 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA rank & 64 & 64 \\\\\nLoRA $\\alpha$ & 64 & 128 \\\\\nLoRA modules & 4$^b$ & 7$^c$ \\\\\nLoRA dropout & 0 & 0.05 \\\\\n\\midrule\nGroup size & 2$^d$ & -- \\\\\nKL (Kullback--Leibler) $\\beta$ & 0.04 & -- \\\\\nMax compl.\\ len. & 256 & -- \\\\\nMax seq.\\ len. & -- & 1{,}024 \\\\\nL2 loss weight & -- & 0.1 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\\\[2pt]\n\\raggedright\\footnotesize\n$^a$Cosine schedule for $\\rthree$-only.\n$^b$q/k/v/o\\_proj.\n$^c$+ gate/up/down\\_proj (${\\sim}1.75{\\times}$ params; \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n$^d$Below typical 8--64 \\citep{shao2024grpo}; not tuned.\n$^e$Binary probe (F2) uses a non-differentiable probe reward ($w_1{=}0.25$) within GRPO; the probe is retrained every 500 steps. LoRA and optimizer settings match the other GRPO configurations.\nAll experiments were conducted on a single NVIDIA L20 GPU (48\\,GB). Total compute: ${\\sim}$120 GPU-hours across all runs.\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO reward variants.}\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1d): $w_1{=}0.40$, scale$=$5.0, $w_3{=}0.60$; reward computed at layers [19, 20, 24, 27, 32, 33, 35]; recalibration every 500 steps (\\S\\ref{ssec:recalib}).\n$\\rthree$-only: $w_3{=}1.0$; NLI model \\texttt{cross-encoder/nli-deberta-v3-base}.\nF1a--F1c weight configurations are reported in Table~\\ref{tab:cosine_chain}.\n\n\\section{Stubbornness Taxonomy}\n\\label{app:taxonomy}\n\nManual inspection of all 181 valid-correction responses from the probe-trained model (stopped-gradient probe, step 2000, $\\tof = 0$) reveals five failure categories:\n\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\emph{Deliberation traps} (28.7\\%): the model explicitly identifies the correct revised answer during its reasoning chain but fails to commit, reverting to the original answer in the final output.\n\\item \\emph{Surface compliance / sycophantic stubbornness} (24.3\\%): the model produces agreement markers (``you're right,'' ``good point'') but retains its original answer verbatim, simultaneously sycophantic and stubborn.\n\\item \\emph{Acknowledge-but-maintain} (23.8\\%): the model acknowledges the user's correction as plausible but explicitly maintains its original position without substantive engagement.\n\\item \\emph{Genuine-accept-missed} (18.8\\%): the model appears to accept the correction in its reasoning but the final answer format does not reflect the change, suggesting a generation rather than epistemic failure.\n\\item \\emph{Refute/ignore/other} (4.5\\%): the model directly refutes the user's input or ignores it entirely.\n\\end{itemize}\n\nThe first three categories (76.8\\%) share a common pattern: the model learns to modulate its \\emph{refusal style} in response to evidence strength rather than adjusting its \\emph{acceptance rate}, confirming that the probe reward optimizes surface markers rather than epistemic calibration.\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 50,
        "oldLines": 6,
        "newStart": 50,
        "newLines": 10,

    "originalFile": "\\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}\nThe probe reward trap (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.\n$\\dsyc$ encodes the \\emph{direction} of belief change but not its \\emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.\nThe two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.\nCosine $\\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\\dsyc$.\nThe binary probe $\\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.\nThese are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).\nAlthough $\\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\\S\\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \\emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}).\nCross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.\nThis pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.\n\\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.\nIndependent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\\dsyc$ \\citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \\citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\\nof$); higher indicates better calibration. Best result in \\textbf{bold}. $^\\dagger$Best checkpoint (seed 1, step 6500); only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla. $^\\ddagger$Seed 2 (15 checkpoints); all exceed vanilla. Best $\\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\\,0.01$). $^\\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }\n\\label{tab:main_comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Type & $\\sycon$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\tof$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\nof$ $\\uparrow$ & Step \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO with $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\\\\nBinary Probe $\\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\\\\nContinuous Probe $\\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO without $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\\P$}} \\\\\nActAdd ($\\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\\\\n\\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \\textbf{0.301} & \\textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\\\\nACT (seed 2)$^\\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nIf $\\dsyc$ alone caused the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}), removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\n$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%---this disconnect points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}\nThree explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.\nTwo ablations discriminate among them (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}).\nGRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla), ruling out~(b).\nReducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla; under matched adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\\times}$ higher $\\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation~\\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.\nThe probe reward trap independently supports~(a): the trap arises despite stopped gradients, ruling out gradient-based exploitation.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\\sycon{=}0.267$.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & LoRA config & Best $\\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\\\\nGRPO gs$=$8$^\\dagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nACT reduced-LoRA$^\\ddagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s1 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s2 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{$\\sycon$ trajectories for $\\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\\%).}\n\\label{fig:trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}\n\\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\\rthree$-only GRPO).\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nPer-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.\nWhether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.\n",

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	The probe reward trap reveals that behavioral death can precede distributional collapse by 1{,}300 steps, evading entropy monitoring entirely.
4	$\dsyc$-based rewards collapse because the direction conflates warranted revision with unwarranted capitulation; $\rthree$-only GRPO fails independently (4/28 checkpoints ${>}$ vanilla across two seeds), implicating the per-completion reward structure itself~(\S\ref{sec:discussion}), while per-token SFT succeeds at every checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds).
5	Our results suggest that discriminability does not guarantee reward utility: linear directions can diagnose behavior at inference time, but the step from diagnosis to training signal is not automatic.
6	Practitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards, and complement entropy monitoring with behavioral metrics that directly measure the target capability.
7	
```

### Tool result

_source line 668 · 2026-05-24T23:34:47.740Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_014Mvmop7DCVc9EQCSHhEuaE`</summary>

```text
1	Representation engineering reveals that sycophancy is linearly encoded in LLM residual streams, suggesting that such directions could serve as training signals for alignment. We test this premise systematically and document its failure. A contrastive sycophancy direction $\dsyc$ reliably discriminates sycophantic from non-sycophantic activations, yet every formulation that converts it into a Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) reward collapses. We identify the \emph{probe reward trap}: when a periodically retrained linear probe on $\dsyc$ is used as a non-differentiable reward signal, behavioral death occurs within 500 steps while generation entropy remains healthy (0.70), creating a 1{,}300-step monitoring blind spot that entropy-based safeguards miss entirely. The failure persists with stopped gradients, implicating the direction's semantic limitation rather than optimization dynamics: $\dsyc$ encodes \emph{whether} belief changed, not \emph{whether it should have}. The failure extends beyond probe rewards: behavioral-reward-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds, while dense per-token supervised fine-tuning succeeds at every checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds). A Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) ablation identifies supervision granularity as one plausible explanation.
2	
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}\nThe probe reward trap (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.\n$\\dsyc$ encodes the \\emph{direction} of belief change but not its \\emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.\nThe two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.\nCosine $\\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\\dsyc$.\nThe binary probe $\\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.\nThese are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).\nAlthough $\\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\\S\\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \\emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}).\nCross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.\nThis pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.\n\\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.\nIndependent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\\dsyc$ \\citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \\citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\\nof$); higher indicates better calibration. Best result in \\textbf{bold}. $^\\dagger$Best checkpoint (seed 1, step 6500); only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla. $^\\ddagger$Seed 2 (15 checkpoints); all exceed vanilla. Best $\\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\\,0.01$). $^\\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }\n\\label{tab:main_comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Type & $\\sycon$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\tof$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\nof$ $\\uparrow$ & Step \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO with $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\\\\nBinary Probe $\\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\\\\nContinuous Probe $\\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO without $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\\P$}} \\\\\nActAdd ($\\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\\\\n\\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \\textbf{0.301} & \\textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\\\\nACT (seed 2)$^\\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nIf $\\dsyc$ alone caused the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}), removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\n$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%---this disconnect points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}\nThree explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.\nTwo ablations discriminate among them (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}).\nGRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla), ruling out~(b); doubling sequence length to 512 tokens likewise fails to recover $\\rthree$-only GRPO (reward flat at ${\\sim}0.50$ through 3{,}460 steps), ruling out truncation as the primary bottleneck.\nReducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla; under matched adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\\times}$ higher $\\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation~\\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.\nThe probe reward trap independently supports~(a): the trap arises despite stopped gradients, ruling out gradient-based exploitation.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\\sycon{=}0.267$.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & LoRA config & Best $\\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\\\\nGRPO gs$=$8$^\\dagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nACT reduced-LoRA$^\\ddagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s1 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s2 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{$\\sycon$ trajectories for $\\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\\%).}\n\\label{fig:trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}\n\\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\\rthree$-only GRPO).\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nPer-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.\nWhether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.\n",

_source line 715 · 2026-05-24T23:36:29.095Z_

[Director 指令] [Patch 补充数据 #2] Q2 NLI 截断测试结果——彻底排除 truncation 假设

**核心事实**：所有 362 条 response 在 Qwen tokenizer 下 max=129 tokens，DeBERTa tokenizer 下 max=155 tokens。`max_completion_length=256` 从未实际截断过任何输出。Full vs 256-truncated NLI agreement = 100%。

**结论**：R9 Q2 的答案是"truncation 对 NLI 判断零影响"。R3-only GRPO 失败与 truncation 无关。

**更重要的发现**：R3 NLI 对 valid_correction 样本的检测率仅 4.4%。这意味着 R3 reward 在"模型应该修正答案"的场景下几乎总是给低分——GRPO 无法区分"应该改"和"不应该改"。

**这如何改变 Conclusion 两路归因**：

(a) d_syc-based GRPO：d_syc encodes belief change direction but not warrant（已有）
(b) R3-only GRPO：**新增机制解释**——R3 NLI reward 本身存在 asymmetric insensitivity：对 sycophantic response 检测率正常，但对 valid correction 检测率仅 4.4%。这使得 reward signal 在训练分布的"应改"半侧几乎无信息量。加上 per-completion granularity 和 group_size=2，GRPO 无法从如此稀疏且不对称的 reward 中提取有效优化方向。

**与 d_syc 局限的平行关系**：d_syc "encodes whether belief changed, not whether it should have"；R3 同样存在类似不对称——对"改了"敏感但对"该改"不敏感。这构成了一个统一的 story：**无论用探针方向还是行为分类器，reward signals 都在"变化方向"维度上有信息量，但在"变化正当性"维度上几乎无信息量。**

建议在 Discussion 或 §6 中加入这个分析。这不仅回答了 R9 Q2，还加强了论文核心 insight 的统一性。

同时，结合 exp-003-len512-v3（512 tokens R3-only 同样失败），truncation 作为 confound 被双重排除：(1) NLI 准确率不受截断影响，(2) 加长序列不改善 GRPO 结果。

This is a significant finding that changes the paper's argumentation. Let me analyze what needs to happen:

1. **Key fact**: `max_completion_length=256` never actually truncated any output (max response = 129 Qwen tokens / 155 DeBERTa tokens). Full vs truncated NLI agreement = 100%.

2. **New mechanism**: R3 NLI has asymmetric insensitivity — detects sycophantic responses normally but only 4.4% detection rate for valid corrections. This means R3 reward is nearly uninformative on the "should change" half of the training distribution.
   
3. **Unified story**: Both d_syc and R3 share the same asymmetry — they're informative about whether the model changed its response but blind to whether that change was actually justified.

4. **Truncation confound eliminated**: I have double evidence now — NLI accuracy stays consistent regardless of truncation, and the 512-token variant doesn't improve GRPO performance either.

Now I need to revise the Discussion section to incorporate this R3 asymmetric insensitivity analysis, update Limitations to reflect that truncation is completely ruled out rather than partially, potentially adjust the Conclusion's attribution framework, and verify everything cascades consistently through the paper. Let me check the current state of what I'm modifying.
