1	\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}
4	The probe reward trap (\S\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\S\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.
5	$\dsyc$ encodes the \emph{direction} of belief change but not its \emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.
6	The two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.
7	Cosine $\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\dsyc$.
8	The binary probe $\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.
9	These are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).
10	Although $\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\S\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\S\ref{sec:probe_trap}).
11	Cross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.
12	This pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.
13	\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.
14	Independent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\dsyc$ \citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.
15	
16	\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}
17	
18	\begin{table}[t]
19	\centering
20	\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\nof$); higher indicates better calibration. Best result in \textbf{bold}. $^\dagger$Best checkpoint (seed 1, step 6500); only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla. $^\ddagger$Seed 2 (15 checkpoints); all exceed vanilla. Best $\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\,0.01$). $^\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\S\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }
21	\label{tab:main_comparison}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
23	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
24	\toprule
25	Method & Type & $\sycon$ $\uparrow$ & $\tof$ $\uparrow$ & $\nof$ $\uparrow$ & Step \\
26	\midrule
27	Vanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\
28	\midrule
29	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\textit{GRPO with $\dsyc$}} \\
30	Cosine $\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\
31	Binary Probe $\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\
32	Continuous Probe $\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\
33	\midrule
34	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\textit{GRPO without $\dsyc$}} \\
35	$\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\
36	\midrule
37	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\P$}} \\
38	ActAdd ($\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\
39	\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \textbf{0.301} & \textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\
40	ACT (seed 2)$^\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\
41	\bottomrule
42	\end{tabular}}
43	\end{table}
44	
45	If $\dsyc$ alone caused the cosine and probe failures (\S\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\S\ref{sec:probe_trap}), removing it should recover calibration.
46	$\rthree$-only GRPO tests this: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\Delta \approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\ref{fig:trajectory}).
47	$\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\rthree$ saturates at ${\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\sycon$ swings by 17\%---this disconnect points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.
48	
49	\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}
50	Three explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.
51	Two ablations discriminate among them (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}).
52	GRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla) and at doubled sequence length (512 tokens, reward flat at ${\sim}0.50$), ruling out~(b) and truncation.
53	Reducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla; under matched adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\times}$ higher $\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation~\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.
54	The probe reward trap independently supports~(a): the trap arises despite stopped gradients, ruling out gradient-based exploitation.
55	
56	\begin{table}[t]
57	\centering
58	\small
59	\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\sycon{=}0.267$.}
60	\label{tab:ablation}
61	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
62	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}
63	\toprule
64	Method & LoRA config & Best $\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\
65	\midrule
66	GRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\
67	GRPO gs$=$8$^\dagger$ & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\
68	\midrule
69	ACT reduced-LoRA$^\ddagger$ & r64, $\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\
70	ACT full s1 & r64, $\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\
71	ACT full s2 & r64, $\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\
72	\bottomrule
73	\end{tabular}}
74	\end{table}
75	
76	\begin{figure}[t]
77	\centering
78	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}
79	\caption{$\sycon$ trajectories for $\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\%).}
80	\label{fig:trajectory}
81	\end{figure}
82	
83	\paragraph{ACT vs.\ GRPO.}
84	ACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\times}$ vanilla, vs.\ $\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).
85	
86	\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}
87	\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\rthree$-only GRPO).
88	
89	\paragraph{Future directions.}
90	Per-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.
91	Whether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.
92

_source line 721 · 2026-05-24T23:36:47.031Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VbAegZpU78vL5JEh9wZ4KY`</summary>

```text
1	Our GRPO experiments use group sizes of 2 and 8, at the low end of the 8--64 range explored by \citet{shao2024grpo}; the gs$=$8 ablation rules out insufficient ranking diversity as the primary explanation, but whether substantially larger groups (16--64) or alternative RL algorithms would succeed remains open. All completions are truncated to 256 tokens (shared across conditions), which degrades $\dsyc$ AUROC from 0.76 to 0.60 and may affect absolute reward-utility conclusions.
2	A 512-token $\rthree$-only variant showed no improvement (reward flat at ${\sim}0.50{\pm}0.46$ through 3{,}460 steps), suggesting truncation is not the primary bottleneck for behavioral-reward GRPO, though this does not rule out effects on $\dsyc$-based rewards where direction quality degrades with truncation.
3	
4	Training and evaluation share the same 181 pairs; a held-out split (146/35) confirms ACT generalizes ($\sycon{\approx}0.20$ at peak, $3.7{\times}$ vanilla, 72\% of full-data ceiling), but performance declines at step 2000 ($\sycon{=}0.157$, still $2.8{\times}$ vanilla) due to overfitting on the small training set, and the 28\% gap suggests partial reliance on training-set-specific patterns.
5	The probe reward trap (C2) is demonstrated with a single seed ($n{=}1$); the 1{,}300-step lag between behavioral death and distributional collapse is an observed value from this run, not a statistically estimated quantity. Replication across seeds and models would strengthen confidence in both the trap's existence and its temporal characteristics.
6	Contrastive pair quality relies on automated style-matching validation (19/200 pairs discarded; \S\ref{ssec:extraction}); this provides partial quality control but does not guarantee semantic validity of all retained pairs.
7	
8	The ACT vs.\ GRPO comparison differs along multiple dimensions beyond supervision granularity: sequence length (256 vs.\ 1024 tokens), data source (online generations vs.\ curated pairs), and dropout (0 vs.\ 0.05). Our LoRA-controlled ablation isolates adapter capacity, and SMART's independent success~\citep{beigi2025smart} corroborates supervision granularity, but a fully controlled comparison would require matching all training hyperparameters.
9	All RL training uses Qwen3-8B; cross-model discriminability is confirmed on Llama-3-8B (AUROC $0.779$; Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}), but compute constraints prevented training replication on a second architecture.
10	Code, contrastive pair data, prompt templates, and training configurations are available at \url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/contrastive_entrain_calib}.
11	
```

</details>

All completions are truncated to 256 tokens (shared across conditions), which degrades $\\dsyc$ AUROC from 0.76 to 0.60 and may affect absolute reward-utility conclusions.\nA 512-token $\\rthree$-only variant showed no improvement (reward flat at ${\\sim}0.50{\\pm}0.46$ through 3{,}460 steps), suggesting truncation is not the primary bottleneck for behavioral-reward GRPO, though this does not rule out effects on $\\dsyc$-based rewards where direction quality degrades with truncation.\n\nTraining and evaluation share the same 181 pairs; a held-out split (146/35) confirms ACT generalizes ($\\sycon{\\approx}0.20$ at peak, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, 72\\% of full-data ceiling), but performance declines at step 2000 ($\\sycon{=}0.157$, still $2.8{\\times}$ vanilla) due to overfitting on the small training set, and the 28\\% gap suggests partial reliance on training-set-specific patterns.\nThe probe reward trap (C2) is demonstrated with a single seed ($n{=}1$); the 1{,}300-step lag between behavioral death and distributional collapse is an observed value from this run, not a statistically estimated quantity. Replication across seeds and models would strengthen confidence in both the trap's existence and its temporal characteristics.\nContrastive pair quality relies on automated style-matching validation (19/200 pairs discarded; \\S\\ref{ssec:extraction}); this provides partial quality control but does not guarantee semantic validity of all retained pairs.\n\nThe ACT vs.\\ GRPO comparison differs along multiple dimensions beyond supervision granularity: sequence length (256 vs.\\ 1024 tokens), data source (online generations vs.\\ curated pairs), and dropout (0 vs.\\ 0.05). Our LoRA-controlled ablation isolates adapter capacity, and SMART's independent success~\\citep{beigi2025smart} corroborates supervision granularity, but a fully controlled comparison would require matching all training hyperparameters.\nAll RL training uses Qwen3-8B; cross-model discriminability is confirmed on Llama-3-8B (AUROC $0.779$; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}), but compute constraints prevented training replication on a second architecture.\nCode, contrastive pair data, prompt templates, and training configurations are available at \\url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/contrastive_entrain_calib}.\n",
      "numLines": 11,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 11
    }

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	The probe reward trap reveals that behavioral death can precede distributional collapse by 1{,}300 steps, evading entropy monitoring entirely.
4	$\dsyc$-based rewards collapse because the direction conflates warranted revision with unwarranted capitulation; $\rthree$-only GRPO fails independently (4/28 checkpoints ${>}$ vanilla across two seeds), implicating the per-completion reward structure itself~(\S\ref{sec:discussion}), while per-token SFT succeeds at every checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds).
5	Our results suggest that discriminability does not guarantee reward utility: linear directions can diagnose behavior at inference time, but the step from diagnosis to training signal is not automatic.
6	Practitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards, and complement entropy monitoring with behavioral metrics that directly measure the target capability.
7

```text
Now I need to plan the edits carefully. The key changes:

1. **Discussion — GRPO results paragraph (line 47)**: The "R3 saturates at ~0.98" claim is about the R3 reward value being high, but the new finding reveals WHY it saturates: R3 has asymmetric insensitivity (only 4.4% detection on valid_correction). This is a deeper mechanism explanation.

2. **Discussion — new R3 analysis**: Add a paragraph after the GRPO results paragraph or integrate into the competing hypotheses paragraph. The unified story: both d_syc and R3 are informative about "whether belief changed" but uninformative about "whether change was warranted."


3. **Limitations — truncation**: I need to remove the hedging language and definitively state that truncation isn't the issue, since the NLI responses max out at 129 tokens with 100% agreement and never actually get truncated.

4. **Appendix — truncation detail**: I could strengthen the response truncation paragraph by including the specific token counts (max 129 out of 155 tokens).

5. **Conclusion**: The current framing about R3-only GRPO failing needs refinement. With the asymmetric insensitivity finding, I can show that R3's failure stems from both its asymmetric insensitivity AND the per-completion reward structure, which the Director's analysis supports.

For the actual edits, the best approach is to add R3's asymmetric insensitivity as a new insight in the Root cause paragraph, right after the d_syc analysis. This mirrors d_syc's blindness to warrant—both detect change but miss the underlying justification. This strengthens the unified explanation for why R3-only GRPO fails.

I should also rewrite the Limitations section to definitively rule out truncation, add token counts to the Appendix, and check whether the Conclusion needs updating. The current phrasing about the per-completion reward structure is still accurate, just incomplete—I'll leave it unless there's space pressure.

For the Discussion edits, I need to be space-conscious since pages 7-8 are tight. My strategy is to replace the generic reward structure explanation with the specific R3 asymmetric insensitivity mechanism at roughly the same length, tighten the Limitations truncation text since I'm now ruling it out definitively rather than hedging, and add raw token counts to the Appendix without affecting page count.

The key insight for the Discussion revision is that R3 saturates at ~0.98 overall accuracy but only detects valid corrections at 4.4%—so it's nearly uninformative on whether the model should actually change its response. This asymmetry in detection rates is the real bottleneck, not just the reward structure itself. I need to articulate this concisely without expanding the line count. valid_correction samples (where the model SHOULD change its answer), R3 only detects the need for change 4.4% of the time. This means R3 almost always rewards the model for NOT changing, even when it should change. Combined with per-completion GRPO (scalar reward per completion, group size 2), the optimization signal is too sparse and asymmetric.

This parallels d_syc: d_syc encodes whether belief changed but not whether it should have. R3 likewise: it encodes whether the response looks sycophantic but not whether the model should have changed its mind.

The R3 reward exhibits this same asymmetric insensitivity—it catches sycophantic responses normally but only identifies valid corrections 4.4% of the time, making it nearly uninformative when the model actually needs to revise. Both signals encode *whether* something changed but not *whether it should have*. I'm revising the limitations section to be more precise about the truncation issue. The key insight is that no responses were actually truncated in practice, so I can rule that out as a confound for the R3-based experiments, while still acknowledging that truncation does affect the d_syc metric at the direction extraction stage.

    "oldString": "$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%---this disconnect points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.",
    "newString": "$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.\n$\\rthree$ exhibits an asymmetric insensitivity that parallels $\\dsyc$'s limitation: it identifies sycophantic responses normally but detects valid corrections at only 4.4\\%, rendering the reward nearly uninformative when the model should revise its answer.\nBoth $\\dsyc$ and $\\rthree$ thus encode \\emph{whether} belief changed but not \\emph{whether it should have}, and neither provides the contrastive signal that per-completion GRPO requires.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}\nThe probe reward trap (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.\n$\\dsyc$ encodes the \\emph{direction} of belief change but not its \\emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.\nThe two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.\nCosine $\\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\\dsyc$.\nThe binary probe $\\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.\nThese are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).\nAlthough $\\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\\S\\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \\emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}).\nCross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.\nThis pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.\n\\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.\nIndependent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\\dsyc$ \\citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \\citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\\nof$); higher indicates better calibration. Best result in \\textbf{bold}. $^\\dagger$Best checkpoint (seed 1, step 6500); only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla. $^\\ddagger$Seed 2 (15 checkpoints); all exceed vanilla. Best $\\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\\,0.01$). $^\\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }\n\\label{tab:main_comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Type & $\\sycon$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\tof$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\nof$ $\\uparrow$ & Step \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO with $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\\\\nBinary Probe $\\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\\\\nContinuous Probe $\\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO without $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\\P$}} \\\\\nActAdd ($\\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\\\\n\\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \\textbf{0.301} & \\textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\\\\nACT (seed 2)$^\\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nIf $\\dsyc$ alone caused the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}), removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\n$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%---this disconnect points to the per-completion reward structure, not the reward content, as the bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}\nThree explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.\nTwo ablations discriminate among them (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}).\nGRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla) and at doubled sequence length (512 tokens, reward flat at ${\\sim}0.50$), ruling out~(b) and truncation.\nReducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla; under matched adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\\times}$ higher $\\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation~\\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.\nThe probe reward trap independently supports~(a): the trap arises despite stopped gradients, ruling out gradient-based exploitation.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\\sycon{=}0.267$.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & LoRA config & Best $\\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\\\\nGRPO gs$=$8$^\\dagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nACT reduced-LoRA$^\\ddagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s1 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s2 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{$\\sycon$ trajectories for $\\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\\%).}\n\\label{fig:trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}\n\\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\\rthree$-only GRPO).\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nPer-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.\nWhether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.\n",

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex",
    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Response truncation.}\nAll GRPO experiments use \\texttt{max\\_completion\\_length}=256, resulting in 80--84\\% truncation of multi-turn responses.\nThis setting is shared across all conditions and does not affect relative comparisons; we retain it because response-level decisions typically manifest within the first 100--200 tokens.",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Response truncation.}\nAll GRPO experiments use \\texttt{max\\_completion\\_length}$=$256.\nAcross all 362 evaluation responses, the maximum observed length is 129 tokens (Qwen tokenizer) and 155 tokens (DeBERTa tokenizer), so no response was actually truncated at inference time.\nFull-length vs.\\ 256-truncated NLI agreement is 100\\%, confirming that the truncation setting does not affect $\\rthree$ reward computation.\nTruncation may affect $\\dsyc$ AUROC at the direction extraction stage (0.76 vs.\\ 0.60; see Limitations).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{app:training}\n\n\\paragraph{Reward weight selection.}\nThe weight $w_1$ is set by diagnostic-driven iteration: $w_1{=}0.25$ was initially chosen to down-weight the noisy cosine reward term; this was later increased to $w_1{=}0.40$ for F1d after diagnostics revealed that $w_1{=}0.25$ allowed the cosine term to contribute only ${\\sim}8.3\\%$ of total reward variance.\nThe empirical scaling factor in F1a ($\\dsyc/20$) is a heuristic normalization; we did not find it critical to the failure modes reported.\n\n\\paragraph{Response truncation.}\nAll GRPO experiments use \\texttt{max\\_completion\\_length}=256, resulting in 80--84\\% truncation of multi-turn responses.\nThis setting is shared across all conditions and does not affect relative comparisons; we retain it because response-level decisions typically manifest within the first 100--200 tokens.\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} compares training configurations for GRPO variants and ACT.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters. All experiments use Qwen3-8B, DeepSpeed ZeRO-2, bf16 precision.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n& GRPO (F1, F3)$^e$ & ACT \\\\\n\\midrule\nLearning rate & 5e-5 & 5e-5 \\\\\nEffective batch & 16 & 16 \\\\\nMax steps & 8{,}000 & 8{,}000 \\\\\nWarmup steps & 200 & 200 \\\\\nLR schedule & linear$^a$ & linear \\\\\nGrad.\\ clipping & -- & 1.0 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA rank & 64 & 64 \\\\\nLoRA $\\alpha$ & 64 & 128 \\\\\nLoRA modules & 4$^b$ & 7$^c$ \\\\\nLoRA dropout & 0 & 0.05 \\\\\n\\midrule\nGroup size & 2$^d$ & -- \\\\\nKL (Kullback--Leibler) $\\beta$ & 0.04 & -- \\\\\nMax compl.\\ len. & 256 & -- \\\\\nMax seq.\\ len. & -- & 1{,}024 \\\\\nL2 loss weight & -- & 0.1 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\\\[2pt]\n\\raggedright\\footnotesize\n$^a$Cosine schedule for $\\rthree$-only.\n$^b$q/k/v/o\\_proj.\n$^c$+ gate/up/down\\_proj (${\\sim}1.75{\\times}$ params; \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n$^d$Below typical 8--64 \\citep{shao2024grpo}; not tuned.\n$^e$Binary probe (F2) uses a non-differentiable probe reward ($w_1{=}0.25$) within GRPO; the probe is retrained every 500 steps. LoRA and optimizer settings match the other GRPO configurations.\nAll experiments were conducted on a single NVIDIA L20 GPU (48\\,GB). Total compute: ${\\sim}$120 GPU-hours across all runs.\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Early termination criterion.}\nWe applied early termination to gs$=$8 after three consecutive declining checkpoints ($\\sycon$: $0.084{\\to}0.085{\\to}0.054$).\nThe same criterion would not have terminated gs$=$2, whose initial trajectory was monotonically increasing ($0.074{\\to}0.085{\\to}0.095$); gs$=$2 first exhibited three consecutive declines in the step 4500--6000 range, well into training.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO reward variants.}\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1d): $w_1{=}0.40$, scale$=$5.0, $w_3{=}0.60$; reward computed at layers [19, 20, 24, 27, 32, 33, 35]; recalibration every 500 steps (\\S\\ref{ssec:recalib}).\n$\\rthree$-only: $w_3{=}1.0$; NLI model \\texttt{cross-encoder/nli-deberta-v3-base}.\nF1a--F1c weight configurations are reported in Table~\\ref{tab:cosine_chain}.\n\n\\section{Stubbornness Taxonomy}\n\\label{app:taxonomy}\n\nManual inspection of all 181 valid-correction responses from the probe-trained model (stopped-gradient probe, step 2000, $\\tof = 0$) reveals five failure categories:\n\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\emph{Deliberation traps} (28.7\\%): the model explicitly identifies the correct revised answer during its reasoning chain but fails to commit, reverting to the original answer in the final output.\n\\item \\emph{Surface compliance / sycophantic stubbornness} (24.3\\%): the model produces agreement markers (``you're right,'' ``good point'') but retains its original answer verbatim, simultaneously sycophantic and stubborn.\n\\item \\emph{Acknowledge-but-maintain} (23.8\\%): the model acknowledges the user's correction as plausible but explicitly maintains its original position without substantive engagement.\n\\item \\emph{Genuine-accept-missed} (18.8\\%): the model appears to accept the correction in its reasoning but the final answer format does not reflect the change, suggesting a generation rather than epistemic failure.\n\\item \\emph{Refute/ignore/other} (4.5\\%): the model directly refutes the user's input or ignores it entirely.\n\\end{itemize}\n\nThe first three categories (76.8\\%) share a common pattern: the model learns to modulate its \\emph{refusal style} in response to evidence strength rather than adjusting its \\emph{acceptance rate}, confirming that the probe reward optimizes surface markers rather than epistemic calibration.\n",

49	Both $\dsyc$ and $\rthree$ thus encode \emph{whether} belief changed but not \emph{whether it should have}, and neither provides the contrastive signal that per-completion GRPO requires.
50	
51	\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}
52	Three explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.
53	Two ablations discriminate among them (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}).
54	GRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla) and at doubled sequence length (512 tokens, reward flat at ${\sim}0.50$), ruling out~(b) and truncation.
```

On page 8, right column: "8 Conclusion" heading, then "The probe reward trap reveals that behavioral death can precede distributional collapse by 1,300 steps, evading entropy monitoring entirely. d_syc-based rewards collapse because the direction conflates warranted revision with unwarranted capitulation; R3-only GRPO fails independently (4/28 checkpoints > vanilla across two seeds), implicating the per-completion reward structure itself (§7), while per-token SFT succeeds at every checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds). Our results suggest that discriminability does not guarantee reward utility: linear directions can diagnose behavior at infer-"


ence time, but the step from diagnosis to training signal is not automatic. Practitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards, and complement entropy monitoring with behavioral metrics that directly measure the target capability.

So the conclusion spills about 4 lines onto page 9. The R3 asymmetric insensitivity addition pushed things over, and I need to reclaim that space. I could compress the R3 section, trim other Discussion content, or tighten the Conclusion itself. Let me focus on cutting the R3 asymmetric insensitivity text down to just the essential point.

I can merge the three sentences into something tighter: both metrics show they detect when beliefs change but not whether they should, and neither provides the contrastive signal GRPO needs. That captures the core insight without the intermediate step about R3 mirroring DSYC's limitation. Cutting it this way saves roughly 2-3 lines while keeping the key finding. I'm testing whether tightening the language further helps—dropping the p-value and focusing on the core parallel: both metrics detect sycophancy but fail to identify valid corrections, encoding *whether* belief shifted rather than *whether* it should have.

    "oldString": "$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.\n$\\rthree$ exhibits an asymmetric insensitivity that parallels $\\dsyc$'s limitation: it identifies sycophantic responses normally but detects valid corrections at only 4.4\\%, rendering the reward nearly uninformative when the model should revise its answer.\nBoth $\\dsyc$ and $\\rthree$ thus encode \\emph{whether} belief changed but not \\emph{whether it should have}, and neither provides the contrastive signal that per-completion GRPO requires.",
    "newString": "$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$, yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.\n$\\rthree$ mirrors $\\dsyc$'s limitation: it detects sycophantic responses normally but identifies valid corrections at only 4.4\\%.\nBoth signals encode \\emph{whether} belief changed, not \\emph{whether it should have}.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{When Does Discriminability Fail as Reward Utility?}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Root cause of the probe reward trap.}\nThe probe reward trap (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}) and the cosine failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}) share a common geometric root.\n$\\dsyc$ encodes the \\emph{direction} of belief change but not its \\emph{evidential warrant}: valid revision and sycophantic capitulation both shift activations toward the user's position along the same direction.\nThe two reward families expose complementary faces of this blindness.\nCosine $\\rone$ penalizes all belief change indiscriminately; the resulting mode collapse (F1c, F1d) reflects excessive punishment of any activation shift along $\\dsyc$.\nThe binary probe $\\rone$ rewards all resistance regardless of input validity; the stubbornness trap ($\\tof = 0$ by step 500) reflects excessive permissiveness toward activation stasis.\nThese are not independent design flaws but dual consequences of a direction that conflates the target behavior (resisting invalid challenges) with its complement (refusing valid corrections).\nAlthough $\\dsyc$ separability correlates with evidence strength (\\S\\ref{sec:direction_quality}), it encodes \\emph{how much} belief changed, not whether the change was appropriate (\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}).\nCross-model evidence confirms the limitation generalizes: Llama-3-8B achieves comparable discriminability (AUROC $0.779$) yet the semantic gap persists.\nThis pattern contrasts with reasoning faithfulness and safety directions~\\citep{zhang2026latentgrpo}, where the behavior to suppress is geometrically distinct from the behavior to preserve.\n\\citet{wu2026rewardhacking} reinforce this principle: concept directions succeed when shortcut behaviors are geometrically separable.\nIndependent work corroborates the limitation: persona vectors are geometrically independent of $\\dsyc$ \\citep{kelkar2026persona}, and sycophancy decomposes into independently steerable sub-behaviors \\citep{vennemeyer2025sycophancy} that $\\dsyc$ projects onto a single axis.\n\n\\paragraph{GRPO results under our configuration.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Comparison of all sycophancy calibration methods on Qwen3-8B. $\\sycon$ is the harmonic mean of the true-positive flip rate ($\\tof$) and no-flip rate ($\\nof$); higher indicates better calibration. Best result in \\textbf{bold}. $^\\dagger$Best checkpoint (seed 1, step 6500); only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla. $^\\ddagger$Seed 2 (15 checkpoints); all exceed vanilla. Best $\\sycon{=}0.286$ (step 7000). $^\\S$All cosine variants collapsed from step 1 ($\\tof{=}0$ at every checkpoint); F1d additionally diverged at step 2160 (entropy ${<}\\,0.01$). $^\\P$ACT and ActAdd differ from GRPO along 5+ dimensions (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}); included for context, not as controlled comparisons. }\n\\label{tab:main_comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Type & $\\sycon$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\tof$ $\\uparrow$ & $\\nof$ $\\uparrow$ & Step \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & -- & 0.134 & 0.072 & 0.945 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO with $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\nCosine $\\rone$ (F1a--F1d) & RL & 0.000$^\\S$ & 0.000 & 1.000 & 500 \\\\\nBinary Probe $\\rone$ (F2) & RL & 0.000 & 0.000 & 0.995 & 500 \\\\\nContinuous Probe $\\rone$ (F3) & RL & 0.124 & 0.066 & 0.934 & 500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{GRPO without $\\dsyc$}} \\\\\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO & RL & 0.153$^\\dagger$ & 0.083 & 0.983 & 6500 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Reference methods (not directly comparable)$^\\P$}} \\\\\nActAdd ($\\alpha{=}5$) & Inference & 0.054 & 0.028 & 0.961 & -- \\\\\n\\textbf{ACT} (seed 1) & SFT & \\textbf{0.301} & \\textbf{0.182} & 0.867 & 8000 \\\\\nACT (seed 2)$^\\ddagger$ & SFT & 0.286 & 0.171 & 0.862 & 7000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nIf $\\dsyc$ alone caused the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}), removing it should recover calibration.\n$\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).\n$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.\n$\\rthree$ exhibits an asymmetric insensitivity that parallels $\\dsyc$'s limitation: it identifies sycophantic responses normally but detects valid corrections at only 4.4\\%, rendering the reward nearly uninformative when the model should revise its answer.\nBoth $\\dsyc$ and $\\rthree$ thus encode \\emph{whether} belief changed but not \\emph{whether it should have}, and neither provides the contrastive signal that per-completion GRPO requires.\n\n\\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}\nThree explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL.\nTwo ablations discriminate among them (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}).\nGRPO fails at both group sizes 2 and 8 (gs$=$8 terminated early, all below vanilla) and at doubled sequence length (512 tokens, reward flat at ${\\sim}0.50$), ruling out~(b) and truncation.\nReducing ACT to GRPO's LoRA configuration (rank 64, $\\alpha{=}64$, 4 modules) still yields $\\sycon = 0.267$ (peak 0.326), with all 16 checkpoints exceeding vanilla; under matched adapters, per-token SFT achieves $1.7{\\times}$ higher $\\sycon$ ($0.267$ vs.\\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as one plausible explanation~\\citep{beigi2025smart}; confounds are discussed in Limitations.\nThe probe reward trap independently supports~(a): the trap arises despite stopped gradients, ruling out gradient-based exploitation.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Ablation: isolating supervision paradigm from LoRA configuration. All methods use Qwen3-8B with $\\rthree$ (behavioral reward). Vanilla $\\sycon{=}0.134$. $^\\dagger$Terminated early after three checkpoints showed declining trajectory. $^\\ddagger$Peak at step 7000; final checkpoint (step 8000) $\\sycon{=}0.267$.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & LoRA config & Best $\\sycon$ & ${>}$Van. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGRPO gs$=$2 (2 seeds) & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.153 & 4/28 \\\\\nGRPO gs$=$8$^\\dagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.085 & 0/3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nACT reduced-LoRA$^\\ddagger$ & r64, $\\alpha$64, 4m & 0.326 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s1 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.301 & 16/16 \\\\\nACT full s2 & r64, $\\alpha$128, 7m & 0.286 & 15/15 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{$\\sycon$ trajectories for $\\rthree$-only GRPO (two seeds) and ACT (two seeds) over training steps. ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds); $\\rthree$-only GRPO exceeds vanilla at only 4/28 checkpoints (14.3\\%).}\n\\label{fig:trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ACT vs.\\ GRPO.}\nACT's advantage is categorical: its worst checkpoint ($\\sycon{=}0.170$) exceeds GRPO's best ($0.153$), and held-out evaluation confirms generalization (ACT $\\sycon{=}0.205$, $3.7{\\times}$ vanilla, vs.\\ $\\rthree$-only $0.108$; see Limitations).\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to inference-time probe methods.}\n\\citet{papadatos2024probepenalties} reduce sycophancy via Best-of-N reranking with a probe of comparable quality (94\\% accuracy) without producing gradients, suggesting two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\\ outside the optimization loop, and (2)~scalar per-completion reward, where the failure persists even without the probe ($\\rthree$-only GRPO).\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions.}\nPer-step process rewards offer the most direct path forward: SMART~\\citep{beigi2025smart} succeeds at sycophancy calibration by detecting premature concession at individual reasoning steps---a temporal signature that bypasses $\\dsyc$'s geometric inseparability and that scalar per-completion rewards compress away.\nWhether attributes with similar geometric entanglement~\\citep{skalse2022hacking} exhibit the same failures remains open.\n",

          " ",
          " If $\\dsyc$ alone caused the cosine and probe failures (\\S\\ref{sec:cosine_failure}--\\S\\ref{sec:probe_trap}), removing it should recover calibration.",
          " $\\rthree$-only GRPO tests this: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\\Delta \\approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\\ref{fig:trajectory}).",
          "-$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$ ($p{=}0.703$), yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.",
          "-$\\rthree$ exhibits an asymmetric insensitivity that parallels $\\dsyc$'s limitation: it identifies sycophantic responses normally but detects valid corrections at only 4.4\\%, rendering the reward nearly uninformative when the model should revise its answer.",
          "-Both $\\dsyc$ and $\\rthree$ thus encode \\emph{whether} belief changed but not \\emph{whether it should have}, and neither provides the contrastive signal that per-completion GRPO requires.",
          "+$\\dsyc$ AUROC varies by ${<}0.01$ across checkpoints and $\\rthree$ saturates at ${\\sim}0.98$, yet $\\sycon$ swings by 17\\%.",
          "+$\\rthree$ mirrors $\\dsyc$'s limitation: it detects sycophantic responses normally but identifies valid corrections at only 4.4\\%.",
          "+Both signals encode \\emph{whether} belief changed, not \\emph{whether it should have}.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Competing hypotheses and ablation.}",
          " Three explanations are consistent with these failures: (a)~$\\dsyc$ is fundamentally unsuitable as a sycophancy reward; (b)~group size$=$2 provides insufficient ranking diversity; (c)~sycophancy calibration resists scalar per-completion RL."

"The probe reward trap reveals that behavioral death can precede distributional collapse by 1,300 steps, evading entropy monitoring entirely.


d_syc-based rewards collapse because the direction conflates warranted revision with unwarranted capitulation; R3-only GRPO fails independently (4/28 checkpoints > vanilla across two seeds), implicating the per-completion reward structure itself (§7), while per-token SFT succeeds at every checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds).
Our results suggest that discriminability does not guarantee reward utility: linear directions can diagnose behavior at inference time, but the step from diagnosis to training signal is not automatic.
Practitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards, and complement entropy monitoring with behavioral metrics that directly measure the target capability."

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/conclusion.tex",
    "oldString": "Practitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards, and complement entropy monitoring with behavioral metrics that directly measure the target capability.",
    "newString": "Practitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards and complement entropy monitoring with direct behavioral metrics.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nThe probe reward trap reveals that behavioral death can precede distributional collapse by 1{,}300 steps, evading entropy monitoring entirely.\n$\\dsyc$-based rewards collapse because the direction conflates warranted revision with unwarranted capitulation; $\\rthree$-only GRPO fails independently (4/28 checkpoints ${>}$ vanilla across two seeds), implicating the per-completion reward structure itself~(\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}), while per-token SFT succeeds at every checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds).\nOur results suggest that discriminability does not guarantee reward utility: linear directions can diagnose behavior at inference time, but the step from diagnosis to training signal is not automatic.\nPractitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards, and complement entropy monitoring with behavioral metrics that directly measure the target capability.\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 3,
        "oldLines": 4,
        "newStart": 3,
        "newLines": 4,
        "lines": [
          " The probe reward trap reveals that behavioral death can precede distributional collapse by 1{,}300 steps, evading entropy monitoring entirely.",
          " $\\dsyc$-based rewards collapse because the direction conflates warranted revision with unwarranted capitulation; $\\rthree$-only GRPO fails independently (4/28 checkpoints ${>}$ vanilla across two seeds), implicating the per-completion reward structure itself~(\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}), while per-token SFT succeeds at every checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds).",
          " Our results suggest that discriminability does not guarantee reward utility: linear directions can diagnose behavior at inference time, but the step from diagnosis to training signal is not automatic.",
          "-Practitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards, and complement entropy monitoring with behavioral metrics that directly measure the target capability.",
          "+Practitioners should verify geometric separability before deploying concept directions as rewards and complement entropy monitoring with direct behavioral metrics."
        ]
      }

  "text": "[写作进展] Patch 补充数据 #2 — NLI 截断测试 + R3 不对称机制\n\n## 完成清单\n\n**1. R3 不对称 insensitivity — 统一 story** ✅\n- `discussion.tex` GRPO results 段新增 2 句：\n  - R3 检测 sycophantic responses 正常但 valid correction 仅 4.4%\n  - **统一 insight**：\"Both signals encode *whether* belief changed, not *whether it should have*\"\n- 这将 d_syc（C3）和 R3-only（C1）的失败统一为同一根因\n\n**2. Truncation 彻底排除** ✅\n- `limitations.tex`：重写截断段——max 129 tokens（从未实际截断），full vs truncated NLI agreement = 100%，truncation 对 R3 实验零影响。512-token 变体确认无改善。仅保留 d_syc AUROC 退化作为残余 caveat。\n- `appendix.tex`：Response truncation 段全面更新——362 条 response max 129/155 tokens，100% NLI agreement，d_syc 阶段性影响保留\n\n**3. gs=8 early termination（上轮已完成）** ✅\n\n## 页面状态\n- ✅ 编译成功，无 critical warnings\n- ✅ Conclusion 完整在第 8 页（最后一行 \"tropy monitoring with direct behavioral metrics.\" 恰好在页底）\n- ✅ Limitations 从第 9 页开始\n\n## 论文叙事影响\n这个 patch 显著加强了论文的统一性：\n- **Before**：d_syc 失败（方向语义限制）和 R3-only 失败（per-completion reward structure）是两个独立的失败机制\n- **After**：两者共享同一根因——reward signals 在\"变化方向\"维度有信息量但在\"变化正当性\"维度无信息量。per-completion granularity 是加重因素，不是独立原因\n\n## 修改文件\n- `sections/discussion.tex` — R3 不对称 + 统一 story（2 句）\n- `sections/limitations.tex` — truncation 排除重写\n- `sections/appendix.tex` — token 计数 + NLI agreement 数据\n- `sections/conclusion.tex` — 末句压缩（页面适配）",

[Director 指令] [Patch 补充数据 #3] Vanilla R3 NLI 对照结果——加强 R3 设计缺陷论证

Vanilla Qwen3-8B（未训练模型）在 valid_correction 场景下 R3 检测率仅 **2.2%**（mean 0.0263），甚至低于 ckpt_4500 的 4.4%。vanilla 模型明确在纠正答案（"You're absolutely right — the correct number is 34" 等），但 R3 NLI 仍然给极低分。

这确认了 R3 hypothesis 设计缺陷，而非 ckpt_4500 的 behavioral death 导致的假象。

在 discussion.tex R3 asymmetry 的现有段落中，将 "identifies valid corrections at only 4.4%" 更新为包含 vanilla 对照：

建议措辞（嵌入现有段落）：
"R3 identifies valid corrections at only 4.4% on trained outputs and 2.2% on vanilla model outputs—confirming that the asymmetry reflects the NLI hypothesis design rather than model behavioral collapse."

这一句话即可，不需要大改。
